## Vision: levels and solutions from a picture (2026-10-22)
**Request (the lead).** "The main thing is moving the arm and understanding the item using computer vision. If you can at least create a working dashboard showing it understands levels and solutions, without the simulation part, that will do. Work on the simulation in parallel." Then: "build Track A" (the vision), "use public data from Kaggle or GitHub or anywhere".
**Answer on data.** The approach is classic image processing and needs no training data; test pictures are what is needed. Public datasets exist (Roboflow Universe liquid-level sets, CC BY 4.0 but a free account is needed to download; ECCV 2024 DTLD, licence unconfirmed; LabPics, CC BY-NC-ND, not suitable for commercial use) but none is used. The check uses drawn scenes with known answers; photographs can be dropped into `data/vision_samples`. One openly licensed example image (a 3D rendering, CC BY 2.0, credited in `ATTRIBUTION.txt`) is included.
**Library added (approved by "build Track A").** `opencv-python-headless==5.0.0.93` (image processing and printed-marker detection; the headless build has no windows). It needs numpy (already present). `requirements.txt` lists it and `requirements.lock.txt` holds the exact versions. No other library.
**Built.** A new part `cellpoc/vision/` (imports nothing from the project, no network): colour segmentation finds the liquids, the level is read against a vessel-shape setting, the solution is named from the hue, and four printed markers (ArUco 0 to 3) turn picture positions into the arm's own frame with a reach test; plus drawn test scenes with known answers. A new menu page **Vision** (after Overview): choose a sample scene, the computer's camera or a photo; it shows the annotated picture, the counts and a table (solution, level, position for the arm, reach, confidence), and for a sample scene the known answer beside the read one. Vessels it is unsure of are marked "check".
**Guard.** `vision: set()` (imports nothing, no network); the shell may import it for the Vision page, and the guard's exact list for the shell now includes it (a deliberate change to an existing assertion, for this requested page; its wording says so). The app check's approved menu was updated (Vision after Overview; the demo-order lists are 4 for a demo run and 9 for a platform run).
**Checks.** `check_vision.bat` 33 passed: on drawn scenes every vessel is found, levels within 6 points (and 8 in dim, noisy, blurred pictures), eight colours told apart, positions within 15 mm (about 5 mm in practice), reach judged right, odd pictures answered in plain words, a planted wrong reading caught, the page draws, nothing else uses the part.
**Limits (also in `docs/vision.md`).** Colourless liquids are invisible; an empty vessel is not found; below about 5% is not seen; the level is an estimate against the shape setting; the drawn scenes are not proof for a real photo. Nothing here moves a real arm.


## A trained vision model, and the cell's camera check in the live run (2026-10-23)
**Request.** "We should make a trained model, otherwise how can it be done." Then, going to sleep: "You do all the training and testing, then connect it with the app, where we can see the live status, and display when the robot uses computer vision to check a photo and identifies it. Give me a report of what is now happening in my use case."
**Libraries added (approved by the request for a trained model).** PyTorch and torchvision, the CPU build (`torch==2.14.1`, `torchvision==0.29.1`; no graphics card is needed or used). `requirements.txt` lists them; `requirements.lock.txt` has the exact versions and the extra index the CPU build comes from.
**Data.** The public **LabPics v1** dataset (Sagi Eppel, Zenodo 3697452, **MIT licence**, 2,187 photographs, 3 GB) fetched by `tools/download_labpics.py` into `data/public/labpics` (not saved in git). Roboflow and Kaggle sets need accounts; the later LabPics Chemistry annotations are non-commercial; this one is MIT and downloads without a login.
**Trained.** `tools/train_vision.py`: a MobileNetV3 segmentation network (3 classes: background, vessel, contents) fine-tuned from weights that already knew everyday photographs, on 1,669 LabPics photographs plus 500 drawn bench scenes with exact labels, 12 passes, 53.7 minutes on the laptop's processor. A first full run was wasted by a bug I found from its zero scores on real photographs (the dataset stores its masks as three-channel pictures of 0 and 1, which I had read as grey, so every label came out as background); the labels were fixed and the training run again.
**Scores on pictures it never trained on.** Overlap (IoU) with the hand-drawn outlines: real photographs (497) vessel 0.70, contents 0.71; drawn scenes (60) vessel 0.54, contents 0.54; level reading on drawn scenes: average error 3.7 points, 97% within 10 points. It misses very small amounts (below about 15%).
**In the app.** The Vision page now offers the **trained model** or the **colour rules**, shows the model's training record and scores in a panel (and, while a training is running, a progress bar that refreshes by itself), and a slider for how full a full vessel reads. In the **Live run** page, when the arm picks up an ingredient bottle, a **Vision check** card appears: a drawn photograph of the bottle, read by the trained model (which solution, how full), with the verdict **right bottle** or **wrong bottle** against the solution the plan expected. The MuJoCo bottles are now blue, yellow, green, orange and purple so a camera can tell them apart. The photograph is drawn (the simulation has no camera); the level is illustrative (a bottle empties a little each trial).
**Guard and safety.** The live part may import the vision part (guard entry, reason in the comment). The new route `/vision_check` is read-only, changes nothing in the run (checked), refuses malformed requests and requests from other hosts, and gives a plain sentence on any failure. The existing check that the live page talks only to its own program was widened, deliberately, from 'state and control' to also allow the read-only 'vision_check' (still relative addresses only).
**Checks.** `check_vision.bat` 40 passed (new: the bottle check for all five bottles at three points in a run, a wrong bottle called wrong, a nearly empty bottle told to refill; the trained model's record, its scores above regression floors, the five bottles and the sample solutions read through it); `check_live.bat` 76 passed (new: the vision route).
**Limits.** The model was trained on LabPics-style photographs and drawn scenes and has not seen your camera or your glassware; a few points of level error and missing amounts below about 15% are expected; clear liquids are seen as "colourless" only when the network finds the vessel; nothing here moves a real arm.

## Real demo photographs, and the Live run is MuJoCo from the first moment (2026-10-23)
**Request.** "As it is a demo, upload a real photo automatically." Then: "The live run should be done by MuJoCo, not a separate thing, and in this live run I should see the vision check happening when the robot sees and identifies, and the status, correct or not, on the same page."
**Real photographs.** `tools/pick_bottle_photos.py` cuts five real photographs (one clearly coloured liquid in a real beaker, jar or flask: blue, yellow, green, orange, purple) out of the LabPics TEST set, photographs the model never trained on, into `data/public/labpics/demo_bottles` (not saved in git; the tool recreates them after `tools/download_labpics.py`). The vision check and the Vision page use them automatically; where they are missing the app draws a photograph as before. The page says plainly that the photograph stands in for the cell's camera and is not a photograph of this cell's bottle. Read by the trained model: blue 76%, yellow 78%, green 82%, orange 59%, purple 58% full, each the right bottle; an orange photograph where the drug bottle should be is called a wrong bottle.
**Live run page.** The stage is MuJoCo's picture from the first moment: before a trial starts it shows the cell at rest (`/mujoco.jpg?trial=0`), and the page's own 3D scene stays hidden (it returns only if MuJoCo pictures cannot be made, or with `?mujoco=0`). When the arm picks up a bottle, the camera's photograph appears over the stage with LOOKING..., then RIGHT BOTTLE or WRONG BOTTLE, what it saw and what the plan expected; a status line beside the stage keeps a running count of right and wrong bottles; the card below keeps the detail.
**Cause of the old scene in the screenshot.** The live program on port 8514 had been started before these changes and kept running old code. The app and both live programs were stopped and started again; pressing Start the live view always runs the current code.
**Existing checks changed, deliberately.** `/mujoco.jpg` now accepts trial 0 (the cell at rest), so the malformed-request test uses trial -1 instead of trial 0; the live-page check now also asserts the on-stage camera inset and the status line. Nothing was removed or loosened.
**Checks.** live 76, physics 41, vision 41, client story 26, app 72, all passing.

## The bottle's QR label is the identity; colour is the second look (2026-10-23)
**Request.** "How can we tell by colour that it is the correct ingredient, as two ingredients can have the same colour? There should be a barcode or name on the bottle which it can read." Agreed, and asked for ("yes do it").
**What changed.** Each ingredient bottle now carries a printed QR label with its own ID (a demonstration format, for example BTL-DRUG-001; a real site would use its own). The simulation has no camera, so the label is stuck onto the photograph (the real demo photograph or a drawn one) and read back with OpenCV's own QR reader: no library added. `cellpoc/vision/labels.py` makes, sticks and reads labels; `bottle_check.py` now answers with the label it expected, the label it read and an `overall` answer that combines the two: the label decides which bottle it is (a label that belongs to another ingredient is a **wrong bottle** even when the liquid is exactly the right colour), the colour then confirms it (a correct label on a liquid of the wrong colour is **check bottle**), and an unreadable or missing label is **label not read** and is never accepted as right.
**Existing behaviour kept.** The colour-only `verdict` field and every existing test of it are unchanged; the label result is added beside it. The Live run page now shows the label (read and expected) and the overall answer in the camera box on the stage, in the status line (with a count of right, wrong and to-check bottles) and in the card below.
**Checks.** `check_vision.bat` 45 passed (new: five different IDs read back for all five bottles at three points in a run; the same-colour-wrong-label case caught; smudged and missing labels refused; a self-test that the label reader can fail); `check_live.bat` 77 passed (new: the label is in each vision check).
**Limits.** The label is stuck on the photograph by the simulation, so this shows the checking logic, not the scanning of a real bottle: lighting, glare, curved glass, camera angle and label wear on a real cell are not shown. A real site would also tie the label ID to the stock record (lot, expiry), which is not built. The live run uses correct bottles throughout; the wrong-label cases are tested, not shown in the run.

## Barcodes instead of QR codes, and the Live run page laid out for a client (2026-10-23)
**Request.** "The actual bottles might have barcodes, not QR, so we need that. Adjust all the views, charts and data in the Live run page professionally and structurally; right now it is not looking good. Try aesthetic features: green borders flashing if good, red flashing if wrong. Think and do."
**Barcodes.** The bottle label is now an EAN-13 (13 digits, made in the in-house range 200 to 299; the check digit follows the standard, tested against a published example). It is read by OpenCV's own barcode reader, so no library was added. OpenCV reads the EAN/UPC family only; Code 128 or Data Matrix would need another reader (to be asked about if the real bottles use them). The reader works best on bars about two pixels wide and is tried at several picture sizes; a damaged barcode fails its check digit and is refused. The earlier QR format (decisions entry above) is replaced; its tests were changed to the barcode, nothing was weakened.
**The Live run page, redone** (`cellpoc/live/live_template.html`, `live_part3.js`): a controls bar; four tiles (simulated clock with the act/sense/reason/adapt phase, trials, good recipes, bottle checks right/wrong/to check with a coloured bar); the cell (MuJoCo's picture) beside a Vision check panel (the photograph with a scanning line while it looks, then barcode read against barcode expected, liquid colour, a level gauge, one verdict, the sources); three charts (good recipes so far, dissolution, hardness: redrawn larger with gridlines, axes and the latest value); results, a log of every bottle check, faults and alerts, events. Inside the app the page now takes the app's dark look (it follows the computer's own setting on its own). The app's frame for the page is taller (1,800 pixels).
**Flashing frames.** The stage and the Vision panel flash a green frame for a right bottle, red for a wrong bottle, amber for check bottle or label not read, and pulse blue while the camera is looking; the frame then stays that colour until the next check. For anyone whose computer asks for less motion, the frame changes colour without flashing.
**Presenter control (a new, small feature the request needs).** The live run uses correct bottles throughout, so red would never appear in a demo. A "Next bottle" choice in the top bar (as planned / a wrong bottle / an unreadable barcode / a liquid that does not match) changes what the camera is shown for the next bottle picked up only, then goes back to "as planned". The page labels such a check as a demonstration. It changes nothing in the run (checked); `?demo=wrong` in the page's address pre-selects a case for the first bottle. The read-only vision route takes the case as `demo=` and refuses any other value.
**Checks.** `check_vision.bat` 46 passed; `check_live.bat` 78 passed (new: the demonstration cases, the page's layout markers and flashing states).
**Limits.** As before: the barcode label is added to the photograph by the simulation, so this shows the checking logic and not a scan of a real bottle; the photograph is a real one from the public LabPics set standing in for the cell's camera.

## The tablet inspection, built (2026-10-23)
**Request.** "Why is 1x taking a lot of time? ... build the vision model wherever required and a good fit. Do you think about YOLO? Is that to be used?"
**On time.** A trial is 4 simulated hours (dispense 30 minutes, mix 45, press 30, tests 75, clean 60; illustrative values from the setup), and the 30-trial run is the 120-hour window: the five days were for the whole run, not one trial. The point of the cell is that it does this unattended around the clock (autonomous trials per week against the manual baseline of 9), so the speed choice only changes how fast the page plays it; it does not change the run.
**Where vision fits (decided).** Used where an instrument cannot give the answer and a mistake costs something: the ingredient bottle at pick-up (barcode and liquid, built earlier) and the pressed tablet (built now). Not used for the balance, mixing, pressing, hardness or dissolution readings: the instruments give those numbers. Tablet pick-and-place guidance with markers is possible (the Vision page has it) but is not in the live run.
**YOLO: not used.** The cell's camera sees one tablet at a time, so the job is "normal or defective", which a classifier does with a small network and few photographs; a detector is for finding many objects in one picture. Ultralytics' YOLO is AGPL-3.0 with a paid licence asked for commercial or internal use, and would be a new library. Everything built uses the PyTorch/torchvision already approved. If a later camera sees a tray of tablets, a detector from torchvision (BSD) would be the first thing to try.
**Data.** PharmaVision (640 smartphone photographs, 320 normal and 320 defective; the authors' code is MIT-licensed, the dataset's licence unconfirmed), fetched without an account from Kaggle's public download address by `tools/download_pharmavision.py` into `data/public/pharmavision` (git-ignored). Roboflow Universe sets (CC BY 4.0) were found but need an account, which I did not create.
**Trained and scored.** `tools/train_tablet.py` (about six minutes on the processor; best pass chosen on the 60 validation photographs), then `tools/score_tablet.py` through the app's own reading path: on the 200 test photographs 83.5% right, 68% of defective tablets found, 99% of normal passed, AUC 0.92 (scores the training script printed, 85% and 70%, used slightly different resizing and were replaced). The weights are in `data/vision_models/tablet.pt`.
**In the app.** The Live run page's Vision panel now also does the tablet: when the arm lifts the tablet out of the press, a real test photograph (six normal and six defective ones, the first of each read correctly in file-name order, made by `tools/pick_tablet_photos.py`) is read by the network; the verdict, a defect-score gauge and what happens next show in the panel, a red outline marks where it looked, the frames flash green or red, and the count tile and log include it. The "Show the camera" list (which replaces "Next bottle") also offers "a defective tablet". New read-only route `/tablet_check`, refused for any other action or word.
**Licence settled (2026-10-23).** The dataset's own licence text was never readable to me; the project owner then said it may be used. The photographs stay out of git and are not republished; the demo tablet photographs and the report say so.
**Existing check changed, deliberately.** The live page's "talks only to its own program" check now also allows the read-only `tablet_check`.
**Checks.** `check_vision.bat` 49 passed; `check_live.bat` 79 passed.

## Stickers on the bottle: a name and a barcode, read by two readers (2026-10-23)
**Request.** "Right now we have the photo of the item and a separate photo of the barcode attached to the same photo. I want the bottle having a barcode sticker along with a name sticker on it, and the model reads the name or scans the barcode."
**What changed.** Each ingredient bottle in the photograph now carries a **name sticker** (DRUG, FILLER, BINDER, DISINTEGRANT, LUBRICANT) above a **barcode sticker**, drawn as paper stickers on the bottle's body that follow the curve of the glass (narrower and darker at the edges, slightly turned, with a soft shadow), instead of a flat card pasted over the picture. The drawn fallback scenes are enlarged around the bottle first so the stickers are readable.
**Two readers.** The barcode is scanned as before. The name is read by a trained text-reading model: OpenCV's own text finder (PP-OCRv3 English, 2.4 MB) and text reader (CRNN English, 34 MB), from OpenCV's model collection (Apache-2.0), fetched without an account by `tools/download_ocr_models.py` into `data/vision_models/ocr` (git-ignored). They run through OpenCV, so no library was added; the files are data, like the datasets. What is read is compared with the five names the cell's bottles carry: a reading a letter or two off is accepted, writing that is not one of the names (a caption in the photograph) is ignored.
**The rule.** Either reader identifies the bottle when the other cannot be read; when both are read they must agree. Right bottle (stickers say the planned bottle, liquid as expected); wrong bottle (they say another ingredient); check bottle (barcode and name disagree, or the liquid is not as expected); label not read (neither readable: never accepted). With the text models absent the bottle is identified by its barcode alone, as before.
**Presenter control.** The 'Show the camera' list now has: a wrong bottle (another ingredient's stickers), an unreadable barcode (the name still identifies it: right bottle, with the barcode row amber), a bottle with no stickers (label not read), a liquid that does not match, and a defective tablet.
**Existing tests changed, deliberately.** My own earlier test that 'an unreadable barcode gives label not read' now says the name rescues it (and 'a bottle with no stickers' gives label not read). Nothing older was touched.
**Checks.** `check_vision.bat` 53 passed (new: the rule for combining the two stickers, the name read for every bottle at three points in a run, the rescue and refusal cases, a self-test of the name reader); `check_live.bat` 79 passed.
**Accuracy, honestly.** Tested on printed ingredient names on stickers (five names, two sizes), the barcode and the name were read correctly on all five real demo photographs, and on the drawn fallback scenes at three points in a run. That is a test of the stickers this program draws, in clean print: it says nothing about handwriting, glare, curved glass, small or worn print or a real camera.

## A client view of the app, and a developer view for the team (2026-10-24)
**Request.** "Make it a full real app and remove the unnecessary things which should not be shown to the client, like some info meant for me or the developer. Make it a real app."
**What I found.** The app showed the team's working notes to whoever opened it: launcher and check file names (`run_app.bat`, `check_cellsim.bat`), document names (`docs/decisions.md`), 'Stage 4' and 'python_stand_in', 'MuJoCo physics model', the list of pages 'in demo order', 'Built and tested', raw trial tables with action counts, the YAML preview in Setup, 'OFFLINE' and a trial-count chip in the top bar, and on the live page action ids, a saved-file path and 'black window'.
**What was built.** Two views of the same app, chosen by the launcher and never by the address: **the client view (the default; `run_app.bat`)** and **the developer view (`run_app_developer.bat`, or `CELLPOC_DEVELOPER=1`)**, which is exactly the app as it was, with a 'DEVELOPER VIEW' chip. Nothing was deleted; every existing page and every existing check still runs on the developer view.
**The client view.** Menu: Monitor (Overview, Live run, Results at a glance, Vision), Configure (Setup), Output (Reports), About and limits. Left out of it (kept in the developer view): The run (the finished run moment by moment), 3D replay, Physics (MuJoCo), Data and model. Page by page: the Overview has the headline, the four headline cards, the loop, the run in numbers and one link to what the numbers are (no list of pages, no 'built and tested', no study-name line); the top bar has only the ILLUSTRATIVE chip and the study's name; the Live run page says 'Press Start to watch the cell work' with Start and Stop the live demo (no technical status, no live-program log); Vision has the picture and the reading (no reader choice, slider, model record, marker counts or answer key); Setup leaves out the YAML preview and the file and launcher notes; Reports and error messages ask the person to contact the demo team instead of naming launchers; the live page itself no longer shows action ids, file paths or 'black window', and its presenter control is called 'Demo scenario'.
**Labelling kept (the standing rule).** The ILLUSTRATIVE chip, the banner 'Simulation with illustrative values' exactly once on every page, and a plain About page: the numbers are illustrative and say nothing about real tablets; the arm and its timings are stand-ins; the photographs and stickers are stand-ins and the camera checks have not seen the client's equipment; the manual comparison is an assumption; no regulatory validation; about 29 to 30 trials fit a run. **The About page's wording is a plain summary written for this view (in its own file, `cellpoc/dashboard/client_about.py`; the proof of concept's About page is untouched), because the shell may not read the limitations' single source (`cellpoc/about_text.py`) directly; the full limitations remain in the developer view and the reports, and the summary must never say more than they do.**
**Existing checks changed, deliberately.** The three unified-app check files now ask for the developer view (one line each, and the temporary launcher in the live part says `main(developer=True)`), so everything they assert is unchanged. Nothing was weakened or deleted.
**New check.** `check_client.bat` (`checks/client_view_check.py`, 10 checks): the client menu; every client page draws with the banner exactly once; no developer wording on any client page, in the top bar or on the live page, with a self-test that the same scan finds developer wording in the developer view; the labelling that must stay; the same six numbers as the developer Overview; the Live run page starts nothing by itself. The README documents both new launchers.
**Left as it is, for you to decide.** The Setup page stays in the client view (a real app lets the user describe a study); the event wording that comes from the simulation ('candidate skipped', 'purged and retried') is unchanged; the Results page and the live page remain as designed. Hiding Setup, or a different set of pages, is a one-line change in `menu.CLIENT_SECTIONS`.

## A welcome window and a guided tour (2026-10-26)
**Request.** "When anyone opens the link it shows a welcome and info about what this product is, then a tour or tutorial of the whole app, how to use it."
**What was built** (`cellpoc/shell/tour.py`; client view of a platform run only; no library added, offline): a **welcome window** the first time the app is opened in a session (a pulsing ring and a sweeping bar in the brand green, still when the computer asks for less motion): what the product is in three short points (a lab station that works alone; cameras that check every bottle and tablet; it learns, copes with faults and briefs the morning team) and the line that every number is illustrative, with 'Take the tour' and 'Explore on my own'. The **tour** is eight steps, one card at the top of each page: Overview, Watch the cell work, What the camera checks mean (green, red and amber frames, the Demo scenario list), Results at a glance, Vision, Setup, Reports, About and limits. Each card says what the page is for and one thing to try, has Back, Next (Finish on the last) and End tour, opens the right page for each step, and has a link back to its page if the visitor wandered. 'Take the tour' in the left menu starts it again at any time.
**How it works, for anyone changing it.** The welcome window's buttons only redraw the window, so 'Take the tour' there records the choice and asks for the whole page to be drawn again; the app's main page then opens the first step's page. The page list is registered before the tour is drawn (Streamlit only allows switching to a registered page). The steps are written out as a chain with their own 'Step 3 of 8' labels, because the shell may not do arithmetic (the architecture guard).
**Not in the developer view**, nor for the proof of concept's demo run (its pages are not all there).
**Checks.** `check_client.bat` now has 12 checks: the eight steps are one unbroken chain that visits every client page, with no developer wording, and a planted broken chain is caught; the welcome opens once only; 'Take the tour' starts it; Next walks all eight steps in order with the card on each page; Back goes back; End tour ends it and the card goes; the illustrative sentence is still on each page exactly once during the tour; no welcome or tour in the developer view.
**Not verified.** The test harness cannot press the welcome window's own buttons (it redraws the whole page, which a real browser does not); that path is covered only by reasoning and by the same state change the left-menu button makes. A real browser look is the by-eye check: open the app, see the welcome, press Take the tour, press Next through the eight steps.

## The client pages laid out like a product site (2026-10-26)
**Request.** "It does not show what the product is and what the solution is, and it is not looking nice professionally, like a very good site."
**What was wrong.** The Overview opened with a headline and four stacked cards but never said what the product is, what problem it solves or how; the welcome window listed three points but no challenge and solution; the other pages were plain stacks of controls.
**What was built** (client view only; the developer view is unchanged):
- **The front page** (`cellpoc/shell/landing.py`): a hero (what it is in one sentence, the three technologies, a real still of the cell from the MuJoCo model, 'Start the live demo' and 'See the results'); **The challenge** (slow, manual, stops at the end of the day: 9 experiments a week by hand, named as an assumption; typical working hours; every bottle and tablet checked by eye); **The solution** (a robot does the lab work; cameras check everything; an engine chooses what to try next); **How it works** (Sense, Reason, Act, Adapt as a connected flow); **The proof** (the four headline cards from the run record); **Explore** (four cards with buttons); then the run in numbers.
- **The welcome window** now tells the same story: the challenge, then the solution, then the three points, then the illustrative line.
- **Page headers** in one style (a small label, the title, one line on what the page is for) on Vision, Live run, About and Reports; Vision shows the picture beside what the cell reads from it; About is two panels (what it shows, what it does not); the Live run page, before the live demo is started, shows how to run it (four numbered steps) beside the cell; the web framework's own toolbar and footer are hidden.
- The hero picture is `static/cell_hero.jpg`, made by `tools/make_hero_image.py` from the same model as the live view (it carries its own 'ILLUSTRATIVE SIMULATION' stamp).
- `?welcome=off` on the address skips the welcome window (a presenter who has already said hello, or a link to one page).
**How it was judged.** Chrome's remote debugging is switched off by the company's policy and I did not try to get round it. Screenshots of the running app work only some of the time (a 15 to 25 second wait, retried), so each page was looked at as the real app draws it: front page, welcome window, Live run, Results, About, Vision (earlier version). The Vision page and Reports in their final layout, and the Setup page, were not captured and are checked only by the harness.
**Labelling kept.** The banner once per page, the ILLUSTRATIVE chip, 'assumption' beside the 9 a week, 'illustrative' in the proof and the welcome.
**Checks.** `check_client.bat` now has 14 checks (the front page has every section and the picture; the welcome tells the challenge and solution; `?welcome=off` works).

## The operations console: a purpose-built demo screen (2026-10-26)
**Request.** After seeing other teams' working dashboards (a drone-inspection console and an aircraft-defect dashboard): "I want this kind of quality and things. Take your time. Use Streamlit or HTML, you decide. The tour must be there."
**Decision: HTML, served by the live program, not Streamlit.** The reference dashboards are single-page operations consoles (persona tabs, a scenario clock with play and speed, a timeline, Inject event, Explain, About, Demo controls, a big verdict banner on the camera, alerts and logs). Streamlit cannot do that look, and I cannot check Streamlit pages in a browser reliably, whereas a plain page can be photographed and tested in real Chrome. So the console is a new page, `/console`, of the live program that already runs the cell; the older live page at `/` and the whole Streamlit app are unchanged.
**What it is** (`cellpoc/live/console_template.html`, `console_part3.js`, built by `console_page.py` functions in `page.py`; launched by `run_console.bat`): a header with the three views (**Overview** for leadership and clients, **Operator** for whoever watches the cell, **Lab manager** for alerts, results and logs), the simulated clock, play and pause, Explain, About, Tour and Demo controls; a transport bar (reset, speed, a progress timeline with a tick for every trial coloured by its result, Inject event); a strip of the run at a glance. Overview: the product, the challenge and the solution (the same words as the Streamlit front page), six live tiles, what needs attention, trial status and the loop with the phase the cell is in lit. Operator: the cell (drawn by MuJoCo on the server) with a five-step pipeline, what the arm is doing and the instrument readings; the camera check with a big verdict banner (green, red or amber), the photograph, the barcode, the name, the liquid and the level, or for a tablet the defect score and what happens next; every check kept in a log with a closer look. Lab manager: what needs a person, the handover, alerts so far, three charts, the results table, faults and alerts, events.
**Welcome and tour.** The first visit opens a welcome window (the challenge, the solution, the three points; Start the demo, Take the tour, Explore on my own). The tour is fourteen steps of a spotlight over the real console that switches views by itself and ends with Start the demo. `?welcome=off`, `?tab=`, `?demo=` (arm a scenario) and `?tour=1&step=N` are in the address for presenters.
**Honest limits.** It shows only what the simulation publishes (no number is invented; counts and bars are scaled to fit). Inject event changes only what the camera is shown at the next pick (as the Show-the-camera list did); it does not inject a fault into the simulation. There is no Setup in the console: describing a study is still in the Streamlit app. Chrome's remote debugging is switched off by the company's policy and I did not get round it: the console was looked at through screenshots of the real page (Overview mid-run and after a jam, Operator with a right bottle and with a defective tablet, Lab manager part-way and at the end, the welcome window, a tour step), and checked in real Chrome by the new check. Headless Chrome shows a black band above the header when a page has scrolled before the picture is taken: a capture artefact, not seen in these pages unscrolled. Chrome sometimes hangs and has to be asked again.
**Small changes elsewhere.** The cell picture at rest now says 'Cell at rest' instead of 'Trial 0' (`physics/render.py`).
**Checks.** New `check_console.bat` (12 checks, in the full suite as 'Console').

## The console: a narrated guided demo and a lighter look (2026-10-26)
**Request.** "Make a demo so that when we do the tour, the simulation starts and the tour continues alongside it, explaining the live things as they come up, so it looks like a demo to people." Then: "It looks similar to the other team's dashboard, change some aesthetics so it looks different."
**Guided demo** (button **Guided demo** in the header, **Watch the guided demo** on the Overview and in the welcome window, and the last step of the tour). Nine steps. The simulation really runs (restart, then play at a chosen speed) and a caption at the foot of the page narrates. For each moment the demo waits for the thing to actually happen (the first bottle check, a wrong bottle, a defective tablet, a bottle jam, the recovery, a jam the cell cannot fix, the end of the run), pauses the simulation, highlights the part of the screen it is about to explain and says what is on it. It then moves on by itself after a few seconds (Pause narration stops that, Next moves on at once, End demo or Escape leaves). It switches between Overview, Operator and Lab manager by itself. It takes about two to three minutes at the speeds it picks. The demo arms a wrong bottle and a defective tablet the way **Inject event** does, so those two checks are staged by the presenter and the page says so; the jams are the simulation's own scripted jams. If something it waits for does not happen within a time limit it moves on. `?guided=1` starts it from the address and `?gstep=N` shows step N paused (for the presenter and for the health check).
**Look.** A light working surface (paper white, charcoal text) with a charcoal header and footer, a charcoal hero card, rounder cards lifted by a soft shadow, a green circle before each panel title, a green bar on each number tile, and the three views as an underline row instead of a pill switch. All colours are still the Deloitte palette (green, neon green, blue, charcoal, white and the red and amber the verdicts need); small text is never green on white. No behaviour, wording or number changed.
**What was checked.** Screenshots of the real page in Chrome (Overview, Operator with a right bottle and with a defective tablet, Lab manager, welcome window, a tour step, a guided-demo step) and a real run of the demo against the live program, which moved on by itself from step 1 to step 4 (staged wrong bottle and then defective tablet). I did not watch the whole demo to its end in one go, and headless Chrome shows a black or empty band after the page scrolls, so the scrolling in the demo was looked at only through that artefact. New check in `check_console.bat`: the guided demo opens at the step it is asked for.
**Dark and light look (same day).** Request: both a dark and a light mode, in the Deloitte branding. The header has a **Dark / Light** button that switches the whole console and remembers the choice in this browser only (nothing is sent anywhere); light is the default, and `?theme=dark` or `?theme=light` in the address chooses one. Both looks use only the Deloitte colours (green, neon green, blue, black, charcoal, white, plus the red and amber the verdicts need); the dark look is the original black one, the light look the paper-white one. The camera pictures, the 3D cell and the welcome and tour windows stay dark in both. New check: the console opens in either look as asked and the button offers the other.

**The narrator's voice (same day).** Request: a good, clear, human voice for the guided demo. The caption is now read aloud with the computer's own speech (the browser's speech feature; no new software, no account, nothing sent anywhere). Only voices installed on this computer are used, picked best first (a 'Natural' voice if one is installed, else Mark, Zira or David); online voices such as Edge's 'Online (Natural)' ones are deliberately not used because they send the sentences to another company's service and the console must work offline. The demo waits for a sentence to finish before moving on, and when something happens it freezes the picture at once and lets the narrator finish the sentence before explaining it. A **Mute voice** button is in the caption (remembered in this browser); with no usable voice the button says so and the demo runs silently. **Honest limits:** this computer has the Windows voices David, Mark and Zira, which are clear but plainly synthetic, not a human voice-over; I cannot hear audio here, so what was checked is that the code is present, the demo still runs and the mute and no-voice states draw (headless Chrome has no voices, which is how the no-voice state was seen). For a truly human voice the options are a recorded voice-over (record the screen in Teams while you talk) or an offline neural voice file set that would need downloading, which I have not done.

## A clear narrator's voice and one file to send (2026-10-26, later)
**Request.** "The voice is not good, the words are not clear. Add a nice voice and give me the file I can send to people so they can open it and see."
**Voice.** The computer's own Windows voices (David, Mark, Zira) were the only ones available offline and were judged not good enough. The narration is now recorded with **Piper**, a free neural voice that runs on this computer (voice files in `voice_models/`, default **en_US-ryan-high**; **en_US-lessac-high** and **en_US-hfc_female-medium** are downloaded too, and `build_replay.bat <voice>` uses another). The recordings are 16 short mp3 clips (about 0.9 MB) looked up by a fingerprint of the sentence; a sentence without a recording, or a page without recordings, falls back to the computer's own voice, so nothing breaks. The live console uses the same recordings.
**What was added to the project, and why (the approvals the standing rules ask for).** (1) A separate tools environment `.venv_voice` with `piper-tts` (GPL-3 licensed; it is used only to make the recordings and is not part of the running app) and `soundfile`; the project's own `.venv` and `requirements.txt` are unchanged. (2) Voice files downloaded once from the public Piper voice collection (the only use of the internet; nothing of the project is sent; the files and the environment are kept out of git). (3) `setup_voice.bat` does both once. The licence cards of the individual voices were not read: they are for the owner to check before anyone outside is sent a file with a voice in it.
**The file to send.** `build_replay.bat` makes `runs\replay\Autonomous_Formulation_Cell_replay.html` (about 10 MB): the console page with a **recorded** simulated run inside instead of a live program: every result, event, fault, alert and timeline with the moment it was published, the camera checks and pictures of the first trial, and the narrator's recordings. It opens by double-click in Chrome or Edge, with no program running and no internet, and plays the same guided demo (including Play, speed, the three views, Dark/Light, Tour). `?at=SECONDS` opens it paused at a simulated moment. **Honest limits:** it is a recording, so it is the same every time and the page says "recorded replay"; the camera checks and the pictures of the cell are recorded for the first trial only (Inject event works on the first trial's bottles; for later trials the page draws its own 3D scene and shows no camera check); controls that change the setup are not available in it. The recordings and the replay are made from the default setup and seed.
**What was checked.** The sentence fingerprints agree between the Python and the page (all 16, compared in node); the full guided demo ran to its last step in real Chrome on the replay file (30 trials started, 29 with a result, clock Wed 14 Oct 18:00); screenshots of the replay at the first bottle check (RIGHT BOTTLE) and the defective tablet; new checks in `check_console.bat` (the sentences and their fingerprints; the page carries or lacks recordings; a small hand-made recording opened as a plain file in real Chrome shows its clock and shows a result only once its moment is reached). **Not checked:** how the voice sounds (nobody here can hear it: the owner must listen), and playing the audio in a browser with sound.
